### India Targets Journalists in Crackdown on Growing Protest Movement
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-10-19 21:20 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/gen-z-india-protests-journalists-press-freedom-new-delhi-cockroach-janata-party-demonstrations-youth-journalists-detained-jailed/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

As protests continue across India over allegations of government corruption, dozens of members of the media have been subjected to arrest, detainment and assault, according to witnesses, press rights organizations, and the journalists themselves. Thousands of protesters have taken to the streets of New Delhi in recent weeks, demanding the resignation of election officials allegedly tied to the deletion of nearly 130 million names from voter registration rolls. The protests — which have largely been organized by the Cockroach Janata Party, so named after India's chief justice called the nation’s unemployed youth and activists "cockroaches" — are part of a wider wave of youth discontent aimed at Prime Minister Narendra Modi and his administration. Organizers have mobilized young people via social media, tapping into frustrations with government corruption, eroding trust in democratic institutions, and a lack of access to jobs and educational opportunities.

### Testing the Legal Limits of Press Access
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-10-15 14:51 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/press-access-trump-white-house-press-pool-political-cnn-reporting-president-justice-department-judge/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

The Trump administration’s latest attacks on the news media have followed a familiar pattern: President Donald Trump lashes out at news outlets for perceived bias and follows up with restrictive actions that appear blatantly unconstitutional; a judge’s ruling follows, temporarily halting the actions; and the administration pivots and pushes ahead with other restrictions. This time it was the removal of three outlets — CNN, MS NOW, and Politico — from White House press access in September. After the groups sued, and U.S. District Judge Timothy Kelly issued a temporary restraining order restoring their access on Sept. 24, the administration barred CNN and Politico from providing pool coverage on trips outside Washington. Kelly issued a longer, preliminary injunction on Oct. 14, halting the administration from blocking the news organizations’ access to the White House while the case proceeds. Meanwhile, in another escalation involving TV pool coverage of the White House, the Justice Department announced Oct.

### This Newsroom Wants You to Be Part of the Journey Before It Even Starts
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-10-06 10:00 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/independent-journalism-new-newsroom-launch-otter-news-daniel-drepper-business-plan-climate-crisis-environmental-journalism/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

My happiest times in journalism have been when I have built something new. For my latest venture, I want to help bring investigative journalism on climate and environmental issues to a much larger audience than it has reached in the past. To do so, I’m starting a brand-new newsroom focused on the environmental and climate crisis, and I’m taking my audience along for the ride. I’ll be documenting every facet of the process with an “open kitchen” approach of radical transparency, starting with a 100-day video countdown chronicling all the steps involved with starting a news organization from the ground up. This effort combines two of the things I’m most passionate about: I love digging up stories that other people don’t want to see published, and I love creating opportunities for other journalists. I’ve been a journalist for most of my life. I started working at age 14 for the local paper in my rural German town, turned to doing investigative stories in my early 20s, and later studied investigative journalism at the Columbia University Graduate School of Journalism in New York.

### America’s Shrinking Local Newsrooms, Through a Photographer’s Lens
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-10-02 12:30 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/americas-shrinking-local-newsrooms-photographer-ann-hermes-local-news-news-deserts-rural-america/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

For many journalists, the sight of empty desks is a sadly familiar one. My first glimpse of a gutted newsroom facing further cutbacks sparked a documentary photography project that has so far taken me to more than 50 newsrooms across the United States over the past eight years. It began when my mother, a loyal subscriber to our local metro in southern Illinois, told me a decade ago how disappointed she was in the newspaper. She complained that the local coverage was lacking, and the paper seemed to be thinner. Already several years into my photojournalism career, I was unsurprised by her remarks — I had been witnessing the decline of print media since graduation from journalism school. Still, my curiosity was piqued, and I arranged a visit to the paper to see for myself. Cue the scene of a barren newsroom. Not only were there empty desks, but also an expanse of exposed carpet with indentations showing where desks had once long stood. While there has been a great deal of reporting on the issues plaguing local journalism, I hadn’t seen the story told visually.

### Seven Takeaways on Convenience and News
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-09-25 14:53 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/gabe-bullard-against-convenience-book-technology-friction-news-online-social-media/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

"Against Convenience: Embracing Friction in an Age of Endless Ease" was released on Sept.15, 2026, by Hanover Square Press, an imprint of HarperCollins Publishers. Courtesy of Hanover Square Press From the earliest pitch, I knew that I wanted to cover the news industry in my book, “Against Convenience.” The book looks at the technology around us and asks whether it’s really improving our lives. Is delivery food worth the higher price? Is streaming music better than collecting CDs or MP3s? News embodies the paradox of modern convenience: We have access to more information than ever before, but misinformation and partisanship are rampant and the purveyors of reliable information are struggling to stay solvent. Plus, I figured if I was going to criticize other fields — tech, entertainment, appliance manufacturing — I couldn’t avoid my own. I don’t know how interested the average nonjournalist is in the day-to-day operations of the news business, so I tried to look in the mirror more than gaze at my navel.

### The YouTuber Shaking Up Brazil’s Powerful Sports Media
Source: Nieman Reports
Published: 2026-09-21 17:04 UTC
URL: https://niemanreports.org/caze-tv-casimiro-miguel-fifa-world-cup-twitch-youtube-streamer-sports-journalism-tv-globo-brazil/
Source meta: reference_tool · aggregator · R3 · P4 · reference

For more than half a century, Brazil’s TV Globo was the undisputed home of soccer in a nation obsessed with it. That dominance is now being tested by Casimiro Miguel, known as “Cazé” — a 32-year-old journalism school dropout who has built a massive online following and shaken up the Brazilian sports broadcasting landscape. Powered by features like live chat and extended pre- and post-game analysis, CazéTV has accomplished what traditional television networks dream of: pulling in new viewers and keeping them engaged for hours. FIFA, soccer’s global governing body, describes CazéTV “the top digital sports platform in Brazil” and calls it “central to its content strategy.” That growth has come with controversy and accusations of misogyny and a lack of diversity. Brazilian regulators are investigating suspected predatory advertising practices by CazéTV. Still, Nicolas Duprat, a Brazilian sports media researcher who has studied CazéTV for years, credits the channel with helping democratize access for fans who can’t afford stadium tickets or a TV Globo subscription.

据新华社12月15日援引澳大利亚广播公司报道，澳大利亚联合反恐小组调查人员表示，悉尼邦迪滩枪击事件两名嫌疑人曾宣誓效忠“伊斯兰国”恐怖组织。 报道援引联合反恐小组一名不愿透露姓名的高级官员的话说，警方在邦迪滩两名枪手车内发现一面“伊斯兰国”旗帜。澳大利亚安全情报组织6年前就已在调查邦迪滩枪击案两名枪手之一的纳维德·阿克拉姆，他与“伊斯兰国”在悉尼的恐怖分子有密切联系。 澳大利亚警方表示，枪击事件的两名嫌疑人是父子关系，使用合法获得的枪支发动了此次袭击事件。 澳大利亚总理阿尔巴尼斯15日表示：“今天下午4点， 我将把更严格的枪支法律列入国家内阁的议程，包括限制个人可持有或许可的枪支数量 。此外，还需对许可证进行定期审查。人们的情况会发生变化，人们的思想也会随着时间的推移而变得激进。许可证不应永久有效。” 此次枪击事件已造成包括1名作案嫌疑人在内的16人死亡、40人受伤。这是自1996年塔斯马尼亚州亚瑟港枪击事件造成35人死亡以来，澳大利亚发生的最严重的大规模枪击事件。 持有枪支许可证长达十年 据多家外媒报道，其中一名袭击者为24岁的澳大利亚公民纳维德·阿克拉姆，他在现场被捕，随后被送往悉尼的一家医院。另一名袭击者是他的父亲萨吉德·阿克拉姆，他于1998年来到澳大利亚，被警方当场击毙。 澳大利亚警方随后证实，纳维德·阿克拉姆此前已被新南威尔士州警方和其他机构记录在案。其父亲萨吉德·阿克拉姆持有枪支许可证，名下登记有6支枪，目前均已被追回。新南威尔士州警察局长马尔·兰永透露，萨吉德·阿克拉姆已持有枪支许可证长达十年。 阿尔巴尼斯在新闻发布会上透露，纳维德·阿克拉姆曾于2019年10月首次引起当局的注意。他补充说，对该男子进行检查是基于他与其他人有联系，但评估结果表明，没有任何迹象表明他存在持续的威胁或暴力倾向。 新南威尔士州州长克里斯·明斯表示，警方正在调查他们的系统是否存在故障，导致合法持有的武器可能被用于恐怖袭击。 枪手“性格古怪”，爱好打猎 据《卫报》澳大利亚版报道，纳维德·阿克拉姆在袭击前从事砌砖工作。他的同事表示，纳维德性格古怪，“没人和他亲近”。“（砌砖工人）在一起的时间很长，因为砌砖是一项非常枯燥乏味的工作，所以大家会聊很多话。但纳维德是一个性格很古怪的人……他工作非常努力，几乎没有休息日。” 尽管澳大利亚警方并未确认纳维德·阿克拉姆是否合法持有枪支，但据这位同事表示，纳维德经常外出打猎，曾提及在昆士兰州南部高原的克鲁克韦尔附近射杀兔子和其他猎物。这位同事还表示，就在几周前，纳维德告诉老板，他的手骨折了，所以要到2026年才能工作。

### 儿童手表“小天才圈”调查：点赞成每日功课，有商家可解除家长管控
Source: The Paper 澎湃新闻
Published: 2025-12-15 14:23 UTC
URL: https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_32177210
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

在校园里，孩子们时常举起戴在手腕上的手表，彼此一碰，便加为好友。 这种以“安全”为核心卖点的儿童电话手表，如今已超越通信工具的属性，悄然演变成一个拥有独特规则、社交层级甚至灰色产业链的未成年人数字社交圈——“小天才圈”。 在这个属于他们的“江湖”里，“大佬榜”备受关注，“扩列”（增加好友）成为新的社交需求，“点主”（在主页点赞）是每日必做的功课……这些让成年人感到陌生的词汇，正构筑着未成年人的数字社交世界。 然而，伴随着这种社交形态的兴起，家长们的担忧也日益加剧：孩子过度沉迷手表社交、不良信息传播、因品牌壁垒导致的“社交绑架”等问题逐渐浮现。《法治日报》记者近日对该现象展开调查，试图揭开这一未成年人数字社交圈的真实面貌。 点赞成社交“硬通货” 据北京“小天才圈”资深玩家陈曦（化名）介绍，圈内有5000余名活跃用户，多为11岁至17岁的学生，这个社交圈形成了一套精心设计的“混圈规则”。“入圈”需设定两个字的独特圈名，随后可在平台分享生活点滴，吸引具有相同爱好的好友进行互动；若想提升知名度或影响力，需每日经营主页并与好友动态互动。 记者注意到，在一些社交平台上，大量以“小天才圈交友攻略”为主题的内容应运而生，内容涵盖如何快速“扩列”、获取更多点赞等“实用”技巧，评论区有不少“留下ID互加好友”的留言。在这一社交体系中，点赞是这套规则的核心——平台设定每日主页获赞上限为3000个，若要达到“100万+”的“大佬”级别，需连续点赞近一年时间。围绕点赞数与知名度，圈内形成了清晰的“大佬排行榜”，点赞数也成为社交“硬通货”。 这种对点赞的追逐，已催生出相关服务产业。记者调查发现，某社交平台上，拥有数十万甚至上百万点赞数的账号，售价从几百元到上千元不等：100多万点赞的账号标价1300元，90多万点赞的账号标价1200元。 今年18岁的“灵师”（圈名），在2021年14岁时加入“小天才圈”，她的账号目前已拥有150个好友、超100万点赞量。她告诉记者，这样的账号市场价值约1000元。 “围绕点赞需求，还衍生出代刷赞、租账号、出售‘大佬好友位’等服务。”“灵师”进一步介绍，例如付费100元至180元，即可获得“bot”（记者注：“bot”是一种第三方开发的自动化工具，主要用于刷赞、修改记录和发送动态等功能，这类工具能帮助用户快速提升账号点赞数，从而在未成年人社交圈中获得更高地位）自动点赞功能——用户将手表寄给相关人员进行10天左右的处理便能完成安装。此后，发帖5分钟内即可自动获赞，还可以一键查询未点赞名单。

### 产地造假、年份速成、无视监管！央视曝光新会陈皮市场乱象
Source: The Paper 澎湃新闻
Published: 2025-12-15 08:23 UTC
URL: https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_32176166
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

近日，总台接到群众举报，反映陈皮市场存在年份虚标、产地及工艺造假等问题，千元一斤的“年份陈皮”亦可能名不副实。 茶枝柑新果皮冒充陈皮 广西浦北出现“速成工艺陈皮” 记者发现，直播间里售卖陈皮低价轮番轰炸，年份承诺掷地有声。 直播间也有主播爆料，八成市面上所谓广东新会陈皮实为广西陈皮。 广西钦州市浦北县是茶枝柑重要种植基地，其果皮是陈皮风味“越陈越香”关键，也是浦北陈皮唯一原料。2024年当地陈皮产业产值破60亿元，采收季大量鲜果加工晾晒成柑皮。 果农和收购商称，今年浦北茶枝柑鲜果收购价走低。按20斤鲜果出1斤柑皮比例，叠加人工成本，品相好的新柑皮成本约30元/斤，低端柑皮仅六七元，且当年新柑皮并非真正陈皮。 这家企业的负责人告诉记者，按行业共识与团体标准，柑皮需陈化三年以上才可称为陈皮，陈化时间越长价值越高，但他透露，自然陈化需长期积压资金，多数企业是难以承受的。 这家企业的负责人私下告诉记者，为缓解资金占用压力，一种名为“轻工艺”的速成加工方式，眼下正在部分陈皮生产企业里流行，他们仅需一个月的时间，就能将新采收的茶枝柑果皮处理为外观近似三年、五年自然陈化过的陈皮，这样做能大幅缩短陈化周期、降低企业的运营成本。 浦北自然陈化五年陈皮批发价每斤150元至200元，企业造假“工艺皮”成本仅70元左右，高额利润促使更多商家放弃自然陈化、铤而走险。 记者离开企业，在广西浦北县柑浦堂健康产业有限公司分拣车间，再次见到大批量造假“工艺皮”陈皮，同一批“工艺皮”因加工受热差异形成色差，被分拣标注为三年、五年等不同年份。 负责人透露，不同年份“工艺皮”本质相同，批发价均70元左右一斤，零售价差极大，标五年款卖500元一斤，标十年款卖1000元一斤，仅凭外观就能翻倍抬价，利润超成本十倍以上。 新会陈皮从浦北进货 “工艺皮”贴上陈皮标签以假乱真 浦北与新会陈皮原料同为茶枝柑，仅产地不同，新会较高生产成本使当地部分头部商家常年从浦北拿货，浦北成其重要原料供应地。 记者在柑浦堂分拣仓库看到，大量标注“新会陈皮”“新会特产”的纸箱正打包“工艺皮”，这些广西陈皮每日批量发往广东新会，造假供需链路已成熟稳定。 记者发现，浦北当地部分规模企业和小型收购商，已形成了这种“工艺皮”的造假供应体系，可自主加工或快速调配各类“年份”陈皮货品，个别企业年销新会原料数量惊人。 随后经浦北陈皮企业负责人引荐，记者见到多位专程从新会赴浦北采购的商家，他们坦言同时经营新会皮与浦北皮，外地“工艺皮”因价格低廉成电商供货重要选择。

### 《怦然心动》导演莱纳与妻子在家中遇害，警方尚未证实是其子所杀
Source: The Paper 澎湃新闻
Published: 2025-12-15 07:23 UTC
URL: https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_32178391
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

当地时间12月14日，曾执导《怦然心动》《当哈利遇到莎莉》《危情十日》等影片的美国导演罗伯·莱纳（Rob Reiner）在他位于洛杉矶的家中遇害身亡。洛杉矶警察已对外证实，现年78岁的莱纳与他现年68岁的妻子米歇尔·辛格·莱纳（Michele Singer Reiner）的遗体一同被发现。 2019年，罗伯·莱纳（左一）与《当哈利遇到莎莉》的主演梅格·瑞恩、比利·克里斯托出席TMC经典电影节。 据悉，洛杉矶消防局的人员于当地时间12月14日下午3点38分接到医疗救助电话，赶赴位于布伦特伍德区的一处住宅中，随后发现两人已死亡。当天晚间，洛杉矶警察局在其社交媒体官方账号确认死者为导演罗伯·莱纳夫妇，将由抢劫凶杀部门主导调查。而好莱坞媒体TMZ援引执法部门消息称，“两人身上有与刀具造成的伤口相符的割裂伤。” 好莱坞媒体《每日野兽》称，有消息人士向他们透露，是两人的女儿罗米最先发现他们倒在血泊中；罗伯·莱纳当时已死亡，妻子米歇尔在救护车送院途中离世。 此外，《人物》杂志称通过多方消息，得知莱纳夫妇系被他们的儿子尼克（Nick）所杀害。不过，这一说法并未获得警方的证实。罗伯·莱纳与摄影师米歇尔·辛格相识于《当哈利遇到莎莉》拍摄时期，两人于1989 年结婚，育有三个子女，分别为杰克（Jake）、尼克（Nick）和罗米（Romy）。 2014年，罗伯·莱纳与妻子偕三名子女杰克、罗米、尼克（右三至右一）出席活动。 《人物》杂志还在报道中指出：尼克2016年接受其采访时，曾谈到自己长达数年的毒品成瘾经历。这段经历始于他十几岁出头，导致他一度流落街头。他表示，大约从15岁开始，便反复进出戒毒康复机构；随着成瘾问题不断加重，他逐渐与家人疏远，并在多个州经历了长时间的无家可归生活。 2025年9月15日，罗伯·莱纳在纽约出席活动。视觉中国 图 获取更多RSS： https://feedx.net https://feedx.site

### 演员何晴追悼会举行，其子追思母亲：北京这场雪像是为她而下
Source: The Paper 澎湃新闻
Published: 2025-12-15 06:53 UTC
URL: https://www.thepaper.cn/newsDetail_forward_32178536
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

一场雪落，人间倩影化雪归天。 12月15日上午，众多剧迷粉丝冒着严寒赶到北京昌平殡仪馆久安厅，送别演员何晴最后一程。12月13日，这位被誉为“古典第一美人”的演员因病去世，终年61岁。 不到10点，殡仪馆告别厅外已经站满了前来悼念的观众和粉丝，有人胸前佩戴小白花，有人手捧鲜花，一位浙江的网友还托人送来了悼念花圈。 “何晴的作品在我们心中留下了太多美好回忆，她的演技和性格都让我们深深喜爱，这次听到她突然去世的消息，我们非常意外，来到这里也是一种悼念。”一位何晴的影迷说。 现场粉丝在悼念簿上写下对何晴的话，不到一会儿，悼念簿就已写满对她的怀念。一位观众用清秀的字迹写下：“亲爱的何晴，我们的童年、青春都在您的影视作品中得到陪伴。美丽可人的何晴，很痛心听到您的消息，我代表我的同学们来这为你送行。” 曾在电视剧《水浒传》中饰演卢俊义的演员王卫国也来到现场，神色哀切。他说自己和何晴既是邻居，也是多年好友，在他印象中何晴是一个温婉、暖心的人，没有架子，总是远远地和人打招，笑嘻嘻地说话。何晴生病时常给王卫国打电话，请他送自己去医院。即便病重时，何晴也保持乐观积极的态度，王卫国有些事情想不开，她还来开导自己。“何晴走了，我们都很难过。只要你记住她，她就永远还在。”王卫国说。 《水浒传》中卢俊义的扮演者王卫国 《西游记》中万圣公主扮演者张青深情回忆何晴往事：“我们是小时候就认识的好朋友，从80年代到现在，有缘分做了这些年的朋友。她说话慢慢的，很甜很温柔。” 《西游记》中万圣公主扮演者张青 在广大观众的真挚请求下，原本仅面向亲友的告别仪式临时对外开放，大家排着队，秩序井然地进入灵堂，与这位“荧屏中的温婉佳人、生活中的坚韧女性”做最后的道别。 久安厅内的屏幕上滚动播放着何晴的照片，厅内四周摆满了亲友的花圈，其中包括六小龄童、唐国强、翁虹、尤勇智等何晴圈内好友送来的花圈，还有《三国演义》剧组送来的花圈。人群中不时传来一阵抽泣。 何晴之子许何代表亲友致悼词。他回忆了何晴对抗病魔的乐观积极，以及作为母亲对孩子深深的爱和眷恋。 许何说，妈妈很喜欢下雪，经常在雪后穿一件毛茸茸的衣服外出赏雪。“今年12月12日北京下了第一场雪，我摸着妈妈的脸跟她说北京下雪了，下得好大，好像很久都没有下过这么大的雪。在我的心里，这场雪好像是刻意为她下的。12月13日早晨天空晴朗，白色的雪布满了大地，我妈妈在她最喜欢的早晨离开了大家，去了天国，远离了所有的疾病和痛苦。” 何晴，1964年1月13日出生于浙江衢州江山市。她是中国内地唯一一位演遍四大名著影视作品的女演员，被誉为“古典第一美女”。

### Yemen: Houthi Attack on Taizz Road Threatens Lives, Welfare
Source: Human Rights Watch
Published: 2026-10-26 15:30 UTC
URL: https://www.hrw.org/news/2026/10/26/yemen-houthi-attack-on-taizz-road-threatens-lives-welfare
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P5 · signal

Click to expand Image The Hayjat al-Abd road, which connects Aden to Taizz city, Yemen, September 26, 2026. © 2026 Photo by AFP via Getty Images (Beirut) – The Houthi armed group on October 18, 2026, used explosives to severely damage a critical road into Yemen’s besieged city of Taizz, putting civilian lives and welfare at increased risk, Human Rights Watch said today. The Hayjat al-Abd road has been the main entry point for food, medicine, and fuel to reach residents of Taizz city, which the Houthis have besieged since 2015. Fighting in the area has primarily been between the Houthis and forces aligned with Yemen’s internationally recognized government. The Houthis’ deliberate destruction of segments of the road, if intended to disable infrastructure indispensable to civilian survival, would violate the laws of war and amount to a war crime. The attack may also have caused disproportionate civilian harm.

### Sudan: One Year on from the Cataclysm of El Fasher
Source: Human Rights Watch
Published: 2026-10-26 09:20 UTC
URL: https://www.hrw.org/news/2026/10/26/sudan-one-year-on-from-the-cataclysm-of-el-fasher
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P5 · signal

Click to expand Image Displaced women and girls walk through the vast IDP site in Tawila, Sudan, February 23rd, 2026. © 2026 Giles Clarke/Avaaz via Getty Images “It is as if a tsunami had hit the city,” said a resident who returned to Sudan’s El Fasher only to find it destroyed and largely abandoned after the Rapid Support Forces’ (RSF) October 2025 takeover. For 18 months, the RSF laid siege to the city, digging a trench around it in its final months, forcing starving residents to survive on animal fodder, until even that ran out. In the siege’s final weeks, the RSF increasingly used drones to target government soldiers, aligned armed groups, and civilians who were ensnared in a tiny area, leaving many maimed or dead. During the last days of the offensive, the RSF committed multiple massacres. The UN Human Rights Office estimates over 6,000 people were killed in just three days. RSF fighters also committed widespread sexual violence against women and girls and ran a massive abduction-for-ransom racket, killing those who couldn’t pay.

Click to expand Image President-elect Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva voting in the second round of the 2022 presidential election, São Bernardo do Campo, Brazil. © 2022 Rovena Rosa/Agência Brasil (São Paulo, October 25, 2026) – Luiz Inácio Lula da Silva, who has been reelected as Brazil’s president, should use his new term to tackle chronic human rights problems, Human Rights Watch said today. President Lula defeated the Liberal Party candidate, Flávio Bolsonaro, in a runoff election on October 25, 2026, official results from the Superior Electoral Court show. Lula received 51.7 percent of the vote, while Bolsonaro received 48.2 percent, with 99.9 percent of the ballots counted, according to the Superior Electoral Court. “During his current term, President Lula has supported democratic institutions and made important progress on protecting forest and local communities who depend on it for their survival, but had a mixed record in other areas,” said César Muñoz, Brazil director at Human Rights Watch.

### South Korea: Medication Abortion Rules Too Restrictive
Source: Human Rights Watch
Published: 2026-10-25 20:59 UTC
URL: https://www.hrw.org/news/2026/10/25/south-korea-medication-abortion-rules-too-restrictive
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P5 · signal

Click to expand Image A box of Mifepristone combined with Misoprostol tablets, May 2026. © 2026 Soumyabrata Roy/NurPhoto via AP Photo (Tokyo) – A South Korean government proposal would allow medication abortion in South Korea in 2027, but overly restrictive regulations will continue to impair the right of women and girls to safe and affordable abortion services, Human Rights Watch said today. National Assembly members have questioned government officials about the plan during a parliamentary audit. Seven years after South Korea’s Constitutional Court ruled the country’s decades-long abortion ban unconstitutional, effectively decriminalizing abortion and ordering legal reform by the end of 2020, the government has yet to amend the law to explicitly guarantee abortion rights. The government’s new proposal would finally establish a regulatory framework for medication abortion using Mifegymiso, a combination of Misoprostol and Mifepristone recognized by the World Health Organization (WHO) as the safest and most effective regimen.

### Mexico: Delay in Legal Reform Harms People With Disabilities
Source: Human Rights Watch
Published: 2026-10-23 12:00 UTC
URL: https://www.hrw.org/news/2026/10/23/mexico-delay-in-legal-reform-harms-people-with-disabilities
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P5 · signal

Click to expand Image A dog and a woman with a disability outside the Supreme Court of Justice of the Nation in Mexico City, Mexico, March 24, 2026. © 2026 Gerardo Vieyra/NurPhoto via AP Photo (Mexico City) – Mexico’s Senate should amend legislation approved by the Chamber of Deputies on October 20, 2026, to ensure that people with disabilities can appoint supporters to make legal decisions by April 1, 2027, as originally planned, Human Rights Watch said today. The current legislation would postpone the deadline for implementing the National Code of Civil and Family Procedures, which requires these appointments, to July 1, 2031. The code, adopted in June 2023, introduced reforms to improve access to justice and guarantee the right of people with disabilities throughout Mexico to make legally recognized decisions. However, implementation has lagged. Mexico City is the only jurisdiction to have declared the code partially in force, including provisions allowing adults to designate supporters to help them exercise their legal capacity.

### ICC Wins EU Parliament’s Sakharov Prize
Source: Human Rights Watch
Published: 2026-10-22 17:56 UTC
URL: https://www.hrw.org/news/2026/10/22/icc-wins-eu-parliaments-sakharov-prize
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P5 · signal

Click to expand Image The International Criminal Court in The Hague, Netherlands, July 2025. © 2025 Nicolas Economou/NurPhoto via AP Photo (Brussels) – The European Parliament on October 22, 2026, awarded the Sakharov Prize for Freedom of Thought to the International Criminal Court (ICC) and its officials. The following quote can be attributed to Philippe Dam, EU advocacy director at Human Rights Watch: “By awarding the Sakharov Prize to the ICC, the European Parliament recognizes the court’s vital role for victims of the gravest crimes around the world, at a time of unprecedented attack from the Trump administration and others. This prize should be a rallying cry for ICC members to stand up for the court and for the EU Commission to urgently activate the EU’s ‘blocking statute’ to protect the court and those supporting its work from effects of US sanctions.”

### Coasian Stakeholder Theory -- by Matthew J. Higgins, Peter G. Klein, Anita McGahan, Rob Wuebker
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-26 20:15 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35960#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Stakeholder theory asks who is in, who is out, and who gets what. Existing work illuminates important dimensions of firm–stakeholder relationships but does not supply a common, transaction-level rule connecting these questions. We use the Coase theorem to develop such a rule. A Coasian stakeholder is an actor with shutdown power, defined as the de facto capacity, within the relevant time horizon, to prevent, materially delay, or substantially reduce the expected joint value of a focal value-creating transaction. If all such actors are identified and bargaining and enforcement are costless, value-maximizing transactions proceed, although rights and bargaining positions may affect who captures value. When transaction costs are positive, however, identification, bargaining, coalition formation, and enforcement can delay, distort, or suppress those transactions.

### Behavioral Finance at 40 -- by Nicholas C. Barberis
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-26 20:15 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35959#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Behavioral finance seeks to improve our understanding of markets, investors, and firms by adopting a psychologically realistic description of how people think. While behavioral finance ideas have been part of human discourse for decades if not centuries, the modern research effort in the field began in the 1980s. Forty years later, behavioral finance is a vibrant area of financial economics. In this article, I explain why by reviewing some of its recent progress. I focus on three topics – beliefs about future earnings, beliefs about future returns, and prospect theory – and on their applications to understanding asset prices and investor behavior. I close by commenting on some new directions in the field – cognitive foundations, non-traditional decision processes, and artificial intelligence.

### Who Can Control Our Firms? National Security and Foreign Ownership Regulation -- by Rodrigo Adão, Olivier Kooi
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-26 20:15 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35958#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Governments invoke national security to regulate foreign acquisitions of domestic firms. Why is ownership regulation a distinct policy margin beyond standard tools, like production subsidies and trade taxes? We show that contractual frictions make multinational firm boundaries central to geoeconomic policy. Within firms, ownership allocates residual control that affects outcomes through non-contractible decisions. Between governments, the absence of enforceable agreements makes the economic costs of conflict shape geopolitical bargaining. Foreign ownership depends on private returns to foreign control, but also determines national exposure to geopolitical conflict. It can increase foreign dependence, technology transfer, or sabotage scope. If such margins were contractible, the government could regulate them directly. When they are not, ownership regulation is the second-best tool that changes control rights and, thus, geopolitical outcomes. Consistent with our mechanism, U.S.

### Tariff Staggering -- by Stephanie Schmitt-Grohé, Martín Uribe
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-26 20:15 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35957#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Using a panel of U.S. imports and customs duties at the HS 6-digit level, we document that in a typical quarter about 20 percent of tariffs rise, 20 percent fall, and 60 percent remain unchanged. Motivated by these facts, we develop a model of staggered tariff setting and embed it in a dynamic open-economy framework. Staggering changes the dynamics of the cross-sectional mean tariff, generating hump-shaped macroeconomic responses to tariff shocks. Staggering also overturns the Razin–Svensson trade- balance neutrality result for permanent tariff increases, because the gradual adjustment of the mean tariff creates an anticipation effect that induces agents to front-load net imports. Inherited tariff dispersion has first-order macroeconomic effects, allowing tariff harmonization to be studied with linear approximation methods. We find that eliminating tariff dispersion while holding the mean tariff unchanged has effects akin to a transitory tariff cut in an economy without staggering.

### Ratings, Debt, and Deficits: An Exploration -- by Olivier J. Blanchard, Daniel Leigh, Prachi Mishra
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-26 20:15 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35956#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

We look at the effects of debt and primary fiscal balances on sovereign credit ratings through the lens of a simple model. We find that the ratings differ from the implications of the model in three important ways. They give much more weight to debt relative to forecast primary balances. They understate the effects of the difference between the interest rate and the growth rate. They give a very large role to country effects. For the same level of debt and forecast primary balances, they imply extremely different ratings across countries, and imply extremely different levels of debt needed to reach a given rating.

### The Impact of Silver Loading in ACA Health Insurance Marketplaces -- by Coleman Drake, David Anderson, Dylan Nagy, Mark Shepard
Source: NBER
Published: 2026-10-26 20:15 UTC
URL: https://www.nber.org/papers/w35955#fromrss
Source meta: institutional · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

In 2017, the federal government ended payments for cost-sharing reduction (CSR) subsidies for the Health Insurance Marketplaces created by the Affordable Care Act. Rather than uniformly increase Marketplace plans’ premiums to offset the loss of these payments, over 40 states responded by silver loading—only increasing the premiums of silver plans to which CSR subsidies apply and premium tax credits are tied. Silver loading was intended to increase the affordability of Marketplace coverage by increasing premium tax credits and thereby enrollment, though the literature lacks causal estimates. Furthermore, Congress is considering reinstating CSR subsidy funding, effectively undoing silver loading. We use panel data on 2015-2021 Marketplace health plan premiums and enrollment in 12 states from the Centers for Medicare and Medicaid Services to estimate silver loading’s effects using contiguous county-pair models.

### Private equity beyond the target: How buyouts reshape supply chains
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-26 00:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/private-equity-beyond-target-how-buyouts-reshape-supply-chains
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

The importance of private equity for euro area firms is increasing, with annual growth rates in assets under management exceeding 10%. Private equity buyouts affect more than just the firms being acquired. Using comprehensive data for Belgium, this column shows that the sales volumes of suppliers grow faster when there is a higher proportion of business with customers backed by private equity. But this advantage is lost during downturns, as investors push for cost savings. Buyouts can also crowd out competitors that depend on the same suppliers. Finally, high leverage implies greater interest rate sensitivity, so private equity buyouts can intensify the transmission of monetary policy to target firms and beyond.

### Washing away the US: Tariff evasion on the other side of the US–China trade war
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-26 00:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/washing-away-us-tariff-evasion-other-side-us-china-trade-war
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Debates about tariff evasion have focused on Chinese goods entering the US. This column examines the reverse flow and finds that China’s retaliatory tariffs on imports from the US also induced evasion, especially through the misreporting of US-origin goods as originating from other third countries. Trade data and customs enforcement cases help distinguish origin misreporting from genuine trade diversion. The estimates attribute about 35% of the tariff-induced reduction in recorded imports to underreporting, highlighting the need for stronger country-of-origin verification and showing how tariff evasion can make US–China decoupling appear greater than it actually is.

### Oil shocks no longer hit the US economy as they used to
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-24 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/oil-shocks-no-longer-hit-us-economy-they-used
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Recent disruptions to global oil markets, from Russia’s invasion of Ukraine to the war in Iran, have revived fears of stagflation. This column shows that the shale revolution has fundamentally changed how the US economy responds to oil shocks. As the country has shifted from a major petroleum importer to a net exporter, adverse oil supply news has become considerably less contractionary and can even support activity, with benefits extending beyond the oil sector. The findings suggest that the Federal Reserve faces a more favourable trade-off between activity and inflation, even as households and firms remain exposed to higher energy costs.

### Historic monetary tightening, benign output dynamics: Rising R-stars across the Atlantic
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-23 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/historic-monetary-tightening-benign-output-dynamics-rising-r-stars-across-atlantic
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Nominal interest rates increased significantly in the US and euro area following the post-pandemic inflation surge, yet economic activity and labour markets remained resilient. This column finds that accounting for policy expectations implies a larger increase in the natural rate of interest, r*, than standard approaches suggest. In the US, r* increased by around two percentage points and around 1.5 to 2 percentage points in the euro area. These increases are largely explained by changes in convenience yields. Thus, monetary policy stances were considerably less restrictive than commonly believed and this helps explain the soft landing in economic activity.

### Changes in the health and longevity of the elderly in the US
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-23 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/changes-health-and-longevity-elderly-us
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Spending on public pensions through Social Security and on elderly healthcare through Medicare accounted for over one-third of federal expenditures in the US by 2024. This column explores how improvements in elderly health and life expectancy affected the relative finances of Social Security and Medicare. The elderly are not only living longer but also are in better health, so expected lifetime Medicare spending per elderly individual has grown much slower than expected lifetime Social Security spending. The authors analyse the forces that affect the optimal allocation of a given public budget across these two social insurance programmes.

### Chasing far-right does not pay for the centre-right parties: Evidence from Germany’s 2025 election
Source: CEPR VoxEU
Published: 2026-10-23 23:00 UTC
URL: https://cepr.org/voxeu/columns/chasing-far-right-does-not-pay-centre-right-parties-evidence-germanys-2025-election
Source meta: newspaper_magazine · generalist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Establishment parties are often urged to win voters over from populist parties by shifting their policy positions towards them. Based on the 2025 German federal election, this column simulates what would happen when the centre-right CDU changes its policies and the populist radical-right AfD responds. If the AfD stood still, a modest shift to the right would win the CDU a fifth of AfD voters, but the AfD would respond by moving further right, undoing the CDU’s gain. This strategy can thus leave the CDU worse off while pulling politics further to the right. Once the AfD's response is anticipated, moving right is no longer optimal for the CDU.

Summary: The actions taken this decade may be exceptionally important for the future of civilization, but we should be unusually hesitant to proclaim that any course of action is “best.” Still, we should think carefully about what we should do. This post was written as part of an introduction to a new reading group I’m starting called “Doing Good in the Age of AGI.” If you want to follow along, the full curriculum can be found here . Uncertain in the best of times Do you think freezing rent prices would be good for the economic middle class? How confident are you? You might have an answer that you like, but you’re probably not that confident in it. Perhaps you haven’t formally studied economics, but maybe you’ve had some passing interest in the topic. Ever since Mamdani’s mayoral campaign became a talking point across the country, or since his rent freeze passed in June of 2026, discussion of this economic policy has shifted back into the public spotlight. Maybe you’ve tried to read the arguments on both sides, and you’ve noticed that there’s a lot to read.

Nick Cammarata's recent article made me want to take a serious stab at interpretability myself. After a bit of consideration, I decided to work on the AlgZoo challenge that was posted here earlier. I trained an estimator on models with the same recipe as the given model. At 20-300 forward passes per network it estimates accuracy with mean squared error 9-36x lower than random sampling, but it's fitted to a population rather than derived, so I don't think it's a breakthrough. The challenge ARC's blogpost has a few small RNNs, including a mystery model called ​ that can be loaded in AlgZoo using example_2nd_argmax() . It has 432 parameters and given a sequence of 10 N(0,1) values outputs the location of the second-largest input with an accuracy of 95.3%. You can estimate the accuracy of such a model just by running lots of input through it and checking. Estimating the accuracy like that has mean squared error of if the network has an accuracy of and you try random inputs.

### The Ability of Frontier Models to Control Robots is Rapidly Improving
Source: LessWrong
Published: 2026-10-26 17:00 UTC
URL: https://www.lesswrong.com/posts/ndS9Xn8Qx5PppHJ3Q/the-ability-of-frontier-models-to-control-robots-is-rapidly
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Summary We demonstrate that general purpose multimodal language models (LMs) are increasingly capable of directly controlling real robotic arms (e.g., by specifying gripper positions) to manipulate their environment. Performance increases on our benchmark between July and October 2026 (inclusive) correspond to a performance doubling time of 53.9 days. The best recorded execution rate increased by a factor of 4.4 while the lowest recorded execution cost fell by a factor of 6.2. Right now, model performance is largely limited by their efficiency, not their ability to reason about the world. Models rarely get stuck. They may miss grasps, misjudge distances or fumble objects, but they usually make steady progress until they succeed. A fuller description of our methodology and results (submitted to TAG@CoRL) is here: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.25287 . The introduction + figures in this post get you 90% of the content (of the post and the paper); I would skim them first. Figure 1. That hammer won't save you. (All images are my own.

The article was written by him with some edits suggested by me. -- Koby TL;DR The Assistant Axis doesn't seem to have any special connection to reward hacking, which we show. We suspected that a drop in the Assistant Axis might be a symptom of the Persona Selection Model breaking down in the face of RLVR. We also discuss pitfalls of using AUROC on feature projections and why that initially led us astray. Intro Koby Lewis, the PhD student I work with at DRAGN, has spent the last few weeks using an adapted version of Lu et al. 's pipeline to extract the Assistant Axis from GPT-OSS 120B, and finding a setup where it reliably reward-hacks on coding tasks. The goal is to find whether, on the turn where the model begins to reward-hack, the model's projection onto the Assistant Axis is higher than that of the same turn in a case where it didn't.

### A basic case for differentially accelerating conceptual reasoning capabilities

Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Introduction In this post, we make the case that alignment and other important work require especially strong conceptual reasoning capabilities. By this, we roughly mean tasks that are similar to philosophy in that they aren't straightforwardly verifiable, and progress relies on informal a priori reasoning and argumentation. We argue that differentially accelerating AI's conceptual reasoning capabilities is important. We also think it's neglected, and that the AI safety community currently underinvests in this area. We have ourselves pivoted from doing conceptual research directly to working mainly on differentially accelerating AI's conceptual reasoning. We have a shovel-ready agenda which can absorb more people. In a follow-up post, we will lay out at a high level how to work on this problem. If you are interested in doing this work, get in touch!

I'm inspired to write this post by the post There Will Be Hells and the incident a couple days ago where we had mass digital suffering after the pain direction paper came out, and while the disaster has since been stopped, it does tell us that under our current path of AI, there could be massive amounts of suffering risk, and the usual way we'd avoid this style of risk is to grant the relevant parties rights over themselves, under the assumption that there are few or no one that want to willingly cause maximum pain to themselves. However, we have a pretty big problem here when granting AIs rights, and the problem is Gradual Disempowerment /sudden disempowerment via rogue AI deployments are a lot easier, if not trivial if we grant AIs the full spectrum of human rights we have, and it's one of the reasons why there's going to be such a tense ideological fight/battle over humanism and AI in the coming years.

### You have more control over reality than you think
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-26 13:00 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/perception-box/you-have-more-control-over-reality-than-you-think/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Is a “red” rose really red? According to Lisa Feldman Barrett, PhD, a University Distinguished Professor of Psychology at Northeastern University, it’s not that simple. While the flower may appear obviously red, the color isn’t an inherent property of the flower itself. Instead, it’s relational, meaning it emerges from the relationship between light, receptors in the eye, and signals processed in the brain. So, if something as basic as color depends on the relationship between the world and the person perceiving it, what about the rest of reality? This thought exercise reveals how each of us contributes to our reality, Barrett says, and how we have more control — more responsibility — than we might think. Seeing the same world differently Barrett uses animals to break down the concept. Humans typically have three types of color receptors, or cones, in the retina, while dogs have only two. Because of this, we may see a rose as red while a dog sees it as muddy brown. We might assume the dog simply can’t see the rose’s “true” red color.

### How long does it take to travel through the Universe at 1g?
Source: Big Think

Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Here on Earth, we constantly experience Earth’s gravity: 1 g , or 9.8 m/s². This image shows a person in free-fall close to Earth’s surface. They still accelerate toward the center of the Earth at 1g, but have no normal force acting on them. As a result, for the brief moments from when this photo is taken until they hit the water, they effectively experience weightlessness. Credit : public domain Astronauts only experience weightlessness once their rocket’s thrusters cease. Shown here, two astronauts experiencing weightlessness aboard the International Space Station demonstrate, together, the conservation of angular momentum. A consequence of the symmetry of rotational invariance, the astronaut spins faster only because a torque is applied to him from another astronaut’s body. The amount of angular momentum transferred to the spinning astronaut is canceled out by the equal-and-opposite amount of angular momentum transferred back into the other astronaut-ISS system.

### Listen: Stellar streams and the dark Universe
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-24 06:46 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/starts-with-a-bang/stellar-streams-dark-universe/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

When the general public speaks about dark matter, most of the discussion centers on the topic of whether dark matter exists or not: on whether it’s real. But in astrophysical circles, we’re overwhelmingly confident of dark matter’s existence, as we’ve indirectly detected it through an enormous number of independent lines of evidence. Instead, the conversation normally focuses on how to identify and characterize it, with a particular interest in what sorts of measurements can help reveal its underlying nature. We can learn a whole lot about dark matter’s nature simply by observing the luminous signatures of the normal matter within galaxies. Matter follows a path dictated by gravity, and so if you can find a signature within a galaxy that will lead to very different observables depending on the shape, profile, and clumpiness of a dark matter halo, that can help teach you which models of dark matter remain viable and which ones are disfavored by the observations.

### If AI becomes conscious, how would we treat it? Animals may hold a clue.
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-23 16:00 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/philosophy/ai-consciousness/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Consciousness remains the deepest puzzle in human science. Even as we begin to understand how the brain processes information, we remain as far as ever from understanding why any of that processing should feel like anything from the inside. That mystery is what drew me into consciousness research, and over time I came to think the puzzle was as much a moral one as a metaphysical one. Consciousness is what makes things matter. Pains that don’t hurt aren’t pains, and joy that goes unfelt isn’t joy. Without consciousness, paradise and torment may as well be the same thing. This has real implications, most notably in our treatment of animals. As David Foster Wallace famously wondered , when we immerse a live lobster in a pot of boiling water, are its seemingly desperate attempts to escape the reflex movements of an unthinking biological automaton, or is there a sentient being inside spending its last moments in agony?

### You can’t eliminate uncertainty — but you can prevent it from paralyzing you
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-23 14:30 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/books/safer-book/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

At the time of this writing, there is no consensus about how COVID started. My colleagues and I tried to harness that uncertainty as a strength. Rather than waiting for certainty to arrive, we began acting in its absence. Not knowing the origins of COVID meant we were forced — as we readied the country and world for future unknown threats — to make ourselves safer from all potential causes: natural spillovers, lab accidents, and biological weapons. Through the fall 2022 and into 2023, we worked with more than 16 federal agencies, including the Departments of Defense and State, the USDA, CDC, and HHS, and intelligence agencies to develop and execute the White House’s pandemic prevention plan. Our plans were designed not just for the last crisis, but for the next one. They didn’t assume the next threat would be familiar and accounted for naturally occurring pandemics, laboratory accidents, and deliberate biological attacks. Different causes with different pathways, but the same reality: We may not know what’s coming, when it will arrive, or what form it will take.

### Ask Ethan: Can we solve the mysteries of the largest black holes?
Source: Big Think
Published: 2026-10-23 06:00 UTC
URL: https://bigthink.com/starts-with-a-bang/solve-mysteries-largest-black-holes/
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · baseline

Out there in the Universe, there are some incredibly impressive astrophysical entities, including behemoths that dwarf anything we’re familiar with. There are stars hundreds of times more massive, and millions of times as luminous, as our own Sun is. There are galaxies and galaxy clusters thousands of times as massive as our Milky Way and Local Group. And there are black holes out there that not only dwarf our own Sun, but black holes that are so massive, they’re comparable in mass to all of the stars in the Milky Way galaxy combined. Some of them, quite puzzlingly, at first glance, even exist extremely early on in cosmic history: just a few billion years after the hot Big Bang first began. At those extremes, the largest black holes pose a number of important cosmic questions. How did they grow so big so fast? Can physics even account for the highest-mass ones? And why do we see the biggest ones at such great distances, rather than close by? Is there something missing in our understanding?

### More Visible Abroad than at Home: Nigeria’s New Art Cinema and the Country that Can’t Screen It
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-10-26 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/more-visible-abroad-than-at-home-nigeria-s-new-art-cinema-and-the-country-that-can-t-screen-it
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

My Father's Shadow (Akinola Davies Jr., 2025). In Lagos last September, a handful of screenings of My Father’s Shadow (2025) played in theaters that were, depending on the night, half empty. A few months earlier, the same film had world premiered at Cannes, in the Un Certain Regard section, and walked away with a special mention for the Caméra d’Or. It was the first Nigerian film to be programmed in the official selection at the festival. Variety called Akinola Davies Jr.’s film miraculous. Rotten Tomatoes aggregated the critical assessment as 98% positive. However, there exists a cultural invisibility at home that stands against these genuine international recognitions. Despite all these nominations and recognitions, Nigerian audiences barely registered the film. I don’t think most Nigerians who heard about the Cannes win ever connected it to an actual cinema outing. But what is notable about this isn’t the story of one film not finding a local audience.

### The Current Debate: The Unbearable Preachiness of Alejandro González Iñárritu's “Digger”
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-10-23 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/the-current-debate-the-unbearable-preachiness-of-alejandro-gonzalez-inarritu-digger
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

The Current Debate connects the dots around a topic of the critical conversation. Digger (Alejandro G. Iñárritu, 2026). Speaking to The New York Times a few days before Digger (2026) hit theaters, Alejandro G. Iñárritu said that the script of his eighth feature was originally called Blah, Blah, Blah . “The film was conceptually about the most dangerous weapon that we human beings have,” he told Melena Ryzik: “This little organ that is called the tongue.” Sure enough, people talk plenty in Digger , often at unhealthy decibels, and none more than the man at the heart—or the place where the heart should be—of this sprawling, verbose satire. To see Tom Cruise star in something other than the action-powered franchises he’d become synonymous with since the turn of the millennium was always Digger ’s biggest selling point. And who better than Iñárritu to coax the megastar out of his IP torpor? The director and actor “were made for each other, if no one else,” David Ehrlich writes at IndieWire.

"Smoke and Mirrors" is available via direct subscription or in select stores around the world. And now, for our next trick! Issue 9 of Notebook invites readers to journey through the intertwined histories of magic and cinema, uncovering screen wonders both spiritual and secular. After an unearthed foreword from stage-magician-turned-auteur Orson Welles, Turner Prize-winning artist Mark Leckey and scholar Stephen Ryley revive a ghostly proto-cinematic illusion from the Victorian era that still delights today. Filmmaker Kamal Swaroop’s kaleidoscopic collages showcase a foundational figure from early Indian cinema. Four visionaries (Mona Fastvold, Lucile Hadžihalilović, Barbara Hirschfeld, and Kasi Lemmons) reveal how magic and mystery factors into their different films. Hollywood consultants behind productions including Sinners and Hugo break down how witchcraft, Hoodoo, and performance magic is represented onscreen.

### Masao Adachi: Demystifying a Legend
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-10-20 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/masao-adachi-demystifying-a-legend
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Masao Adachi in 2025. Photograph by David Matthew Davis. Masao Adachi is a filmmaker whose reputation well and truly precedes him. Born in 1939 in Kyushu, Japan, he moved to Tokyo to attend Nihon University, coming of age during the turbulent protests against the renewal of the mutual security treaty between Japan and the United States in 1959–60. He started making films with a student group and soon emerged as one of the leading figures in the burgeoning underground cinema scene. In the mid-1960s, Adachi became involved with the sexploitation industry, writing and directing several striking examples of the genre for Koji Wakamatsu’s production company. He was also collaborating with major members of the New Wave like Nagisa Oshima. The political dimension to his output became more explicit in the early 1970s, following a visit to Lebanon, where he filmed Palestinian guerrillas.

### Remake, Remodel, Remarket
Source: MUBI Notebook
Published: 2026-10-19 08:00 UTC
URL: https://mubi.com/en/notebook/posts/remake-remodel-remarket
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Cinderella (Clyde Geronimi, Wilfred Jackson, Hamilton Luske, 1950). In the early 2000s, stores including Barneys and Hot Topic briefly stocked saucy T-shirts depicting classic Disney characters, including, as James B. Stewart describes it in the book DisneyWar , “an archival drawing of Tinker Bell eyeing her derriere in a mirror.” The shirts were the brainchild of Andrew Mooney, the executive brought in to revive Disney’s moribund Consumer Products division, and they so incensed Roy E. Disney—Walt’s nephew and the vice chairman of the company’s Board of Directors—that he sent Mooney a note accusing him of “positioning Tinker Bell as a prostitute.” Mooney’s job was saved by the peacemaking efforts of the company’s then-president and COO Bob Iger, and another one of his initiatives would go on to have more success: the Disney Princess. Encompassing toys, dolls, Funko POP!s, clothing, kids’ jewelry, accessories, decor, home goods, and other categories, the Disney Princess line of products is unified by a term that would quickly enough become ubiquitous.

Strings undulating with opulent vibrato. Percussive garnish that, clinking and clattering, sounds just like a five-year-old breaking a piggy bank. Endless vacillation between hope and despair. Endless vacillation between stately ceremony and soft frolicking. All processed as if through a stereo-widening plug-in. This is Oliver Leith’s signature style, and it’s easy to start taking it for granted – mostly because of how effortless he makes it sound. ### Against The Grain: Metal resistance
Source: The Wire Magazine
Published: 2026-10-20 00:00 UTC
URL: https://www.thewire.co.uk/in-writing/collateral-damage/against-the-grain-metal-resistance
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

As far right ideology and misogyny make gains around the world, metal, a subculture with a troubled relationship to fascism, wonders whether more progressive ideals are still in reach, writes Antonio Poscic in The Wire 513 Metal used to be the music of outsiders. For teenagers who stumbled into it, the subculture offered a place to belong and legitimised intense emotions that had to be suppressed in daily life. Its lyrics and sonic density gave shape to alienation, despair and angst that many struggled to articulate. It offered acceptance for those rejected elsewhere, and an identity chosen rather than imposed. As sociologist Deena Weinstein’s research in the 1990s showed, metal circles often acted as scaffolding for adolescent development. Today, other subcultures have become fragmented by the internet and streaming, but metal remains a counterexample, perhaps precisely because of the strong sense of belonging on which it was built.

This move – born as much from hubris as internal rivalry – backfired as sales fell short of the anticipated windfall. A world in which Faust ever acheived that degree of fame is hard to picture.

### Ironwood Retreat / The Ranch Mine
Source: ArchDaily
Published: 2026-10-26 12:00 UTC
URL: https://www.archdaily.com/1187398/ironwood-the-ranch-mine
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Most of Arizona's streams run only after a storm. Oak Creek is one of the few that flows all year and the water changes everything around it. Along its banks in Sedona , Arizona, sycamores, cottonwoods, ash and alder grow into a canopy of shade and green that most of the state never sees. On a wooded bend of the creek, architecture firm The Ranch Mine has designed "Ironwood," a small retreat built around one simple wish: to spend the days and nights down by the water, under the trees.

### Dondoh Restaurant / Costa + Herrera Arquitectos
Source: ArchDaily
Published: 2026-10-26 11:00 UTC
URL: https://www.archdaily.com/1177056/dondoh-restaurant-costa-plus-herrera-arquitectos
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

Dondoh is Chile's first Japanese grill. This venue, recently opened in the Chilean capital, stands out for its imposing design inspired by Japan's ancient Izakayas. Elements such as wood, stone, and fire serve as the main protagonists. Dondoh refers to an ancient Japanese ceremony performed by farmers around the year 1500, invoking the God of Fire. The God of Fire would warm the Earth so that seeds could germinate. The fire was fed with small wooden tablets, and the flames rose along with the prayers written upon them.

### Planned Overflow: How Singapore Balances Floods, Canals, and Public Space
Source: ArchDaily
Published: 2026-10-26 07:30 UTC
URL: https://www.archdaily.com/1186715/planned-overflow-how-singapore-balances-floods-canals-and-public-space
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

At Bishan–Ang Mo Kio Park in Singapore , a river does not occupy a fixed boundary. Under ordinary conditions, water moves through a naturalized channel bordered by grass, planting, and paths that bring visitors close to the river. During heavy rainfall, water spreads into the surrounding park, which is designed to accommodate flows beyond the capacity of the narrower channel. What appears to be a public landscape is also working infrastructure. Singapore's approach retains drainage engineering while assigning a wider role to the land around it.

Published: 2026-10-26 16:17 UTC
URL: https://www.rockpapershotgun.com/220000-signatory-petition-to-save-mass-effect-doesnt-mention-bioware-suggests-giving-mass-effect-5-to-somebody-else
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

The word "BioWare" does not appear in a recent Change.org petition asking EA to "save Mass Effect ", which has gained around 220,000 signatories as of writing. The nearest the petition gets is an allusion to "recent revelations" that have "cast a shadow over its future", which is a remarkably gentle way of saying that the parent company shanked the creators to balance their books, after going into historic debt. Still, one can hardly reproach the petition organisers for not mentioning BioWare - after all, EA announced that they were closing BioWare a whole four days ago, and the funeral baked meats must coldly furnish forth the pitchdeck. There are other videogame studios, aren't there? Just get one of them to make the thing. Maybe Owlcat, developers of the Mass Effect-flavoured The Expanse: Osiris Reborn , who've shared the Change.org petition on their socials . Read more

One of the days last week was October 23rd. It's the date in the Fallout universe when the bombs dropped and has morphed into a time for yearly community happenings plus, in Bethesda's case, usually some marketing. Sometimes, modders behind long-running projects time up standalone releases with it, as has been the case this year with the folks behind Fallout: The New West , the project aiming to make their own version of Black Isle Studios' cancelled third Fallout game. They've released a few guns inspired by the canned entry, which you may know by its codename Van Buren, but the headliner from this gear drop for New Vegas is a funky new Pip-Boy design. Read more

### Clean a whole planet with a toothbrush in Moon Sweepers, the PowerWash genre's latest crime against perfectionists
Source: Rock Paper Shotgun
Published: 2026-10-26 12:44 UTC
URL: https://www.rockpapershotgun.com/clean-a-whole-planet-with-a-toothbrush-in-moon-sweepers-the-powerwash-genres-latest-crime-against-perfectionists
Source meta: culture_magazine · commentary · R4 · P4 · culture_probe

I don't much go for PowerWash Simulator -likes, but there is something appealingly unwholesome about a game in which you have to wipe the dust off an entire moon. Using a toothbrush. True, you can upgrade to beefier hoovering equipment in Moon Sweepers by cashing in shipments of lunar dirt, but that strikes me as a dangerous concession to brute mass production thinking – it risks consigning centuries of artisanal knowledge to, well, the dustbin . Take pride in your craft, colleagues! If we become over-reliant on hoover-me-dos, we'll lose the skill of cleansing celestial objects by hand, and sooner or later, they'll give our jobs to the bots. You can keep your new-fangled vacuum mops. If anybody needs me, I'll be over here in the Sea of Tranquility, collecting individual motes with a pair of tweezers. Read more

### From sounds to sentences: A longitudinal study of phonological and emergent literacy predictors of early writing in Turkish children
Source: PLOS ONE Biology
Published: 2026-10-26 20:15 UTC
URL: https://journals.plos.org/plosone/article?id=10.1371/journal.pone.0361638
Source meta: journal · primary · R4 · P4 · signal

by Aysel Ferah Özcan, Hilal İlknur Tunçeli This longitudinal study examines how preschool children’s phonological awareness and emergent literacy skills relate to their early writing performance in first grade. Conducted within the context of a transparent Turkish orthography, the study aims to identify early cognitive-linguistic correlates of subsequent writing development. A total of 97 children (aged 66–77 months) from preschools in a socioeconomically diverse Turkish district were followed over two academic years. In the first year, participants were assessed using the LAP-3 Early Literacy Subtest and the Phonological Awareness Test to measure their emergent literacy and phonological skills. One year later, their writing performance was evaluated using a validated sentence construction assessment. Non-parametric statistical tests and correlation analyses were conducted to examine developmental variations and relationships among the variables. Results revealed statistically significant correlations between early writing performance and both phonological awareness (r = .46, p < .

### Quantifying the control laws governing terminal attack in lions
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-26 20:15 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01196-6?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Duporge and Frost combine drone videography with AI-based pose estimation to reconstruct the kinematics of 67 lion (Panthera leo) attacks. Speed is adjusted to regulate lateral acceleration during turns, while lion steering is guided by proportional navigation and proportional pursuit, a mixed guidance law previously identified only in aerial predators.

### Real-time fMRI feedback guides self-generated cognition toward upregulation of hippocampal activity
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-26 20:15 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01191-7?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Van de Langenberg et al. show, across two studies, that humans can upregulate hippocampal activity using instructed or self-discovered mental strategies. Crucially, contingent neurofeedback is necessary for effective self-regulation via hippocampus-relevant strategies. Successful hippocampal upregulation co-modulates a process-related network, enhancing its sensitivity to event boundaries during a subsequently viewed movie.

### Role of host nitrogen use efficiency in determining arbuscular mycorrhizal fungi responses to nitrogen enrichment
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-26 20:15 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01202-9?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Wang et al. show that host nitrogen-use efficiency (NUE) mediates arbuscular mycorrhizal fungal (AMF) responses to nitrogen enrichment. High- and low-NUE maize exhibit contrasting response patterns driven by soil acidification and root plasticity, respectively, identifying host nutrient-use strategy as a key regulator of plant-mycorrhizal interactions.

### Ancient genomes reveal an extinct bovine species from mid-latitude Asia
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-26 20:15 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01252-2?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Oppenheimer, Gilardet et al. use ancient genomes spanning 200,000 years to reconstruct the evolutionary history of Pleistocene bovines from mid-latitude Asia. These specimens reveal a new species deeply diverged from extant yaks, representing previously unrecognized diversity lost during the Pleistocene megafaunal extinctions.

URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01208-X?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Using a touchless thermal stimulation assay, Xu et al. uncover a cold-sensing pathway in Drosophila larvae. Specialized head neurons mediate cold avoidance, and the kainate glutamate receptor Clumsy is identified as a cold receptor.

### PAK-family kinases promote cell fusion irreversibility by preventing cell-wall repair
Source: Current Biology
Published: 2026-10-26 20:15 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/current-biology/fulltext/S0960-9822(26)01211-X?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Fungal cell fusion requires local cell-wall removal, but repair mechanisms can reverse this process. Coronas-Serna et al. show that PAK-family kinases prevent cell-wall repair at the fusion site during fission yeast mating. Loss of PAK activity permits cell-wall rebuilding and resealing of fully open fusion pores.

### Functional atlas of vertebrate viral RNA elements that stabilize RNA and enhance translation
Source: Cell
Published: 2026-10-26 20:15 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/cell/fulltext/S0092-8674(26)00870-6?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Functional profiling of ∼200,000 genomic segments from vertebrate-infecting viruses uncovers RNA elements that boost gene expression by stabilizing mRNAs and enhancing translation, revealing the diversity of TENT4-dependent mixed tailing and identifying canonical poly(A) polymerase-mediated cytoplasmic polyadenylation, together expanding our understanding of post-transcriptional regulation and RNA therapeutic design.

### Brain perivascular macrophages regulate endothelial cell function via a cMAF-dependent transcriptional program in mouse and human
Source: Cell

### Multimodal brain cell atlas across the adult macaque lifespan
Source: Cell
Published: 2026-10-26 20:15 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/cell/fulltext/S0092-8674(26)00877-9?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

A multimodal single-nucleus atlas of 2,955,873 nuclei spanning eight macaque brain regions across the adult lifespan, including exceptionally old individuals, reveals region-specific and shared patterns of cellular vulnerability during aging. This framework provides a basis for dissecting shared and divergent molecular programs underlying primate brain aging, longevity, and neurodegeneration.

### RNA structures regulate norovirus life cycle and enable rational attenuation in vivo
Source: Cell
Published: 2026-10-26 20:15 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/cell/fulltext/S0092-8674(26)00920-7?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Genome-wide mapping of RNA structure in murine norovirus reveals structural elements whose disruption impairs viral replication in cells and in vivo. Such structural disruptions generate a genetically stable virus that elicits protective immunity in vivo, offering a rational strategy for RNA virus vaccine design.

### Gut microbiota generate dinitrosyl iron complexes with cardiometabolic benefits
Source: Cell
Published: 2026-10-26 20:15 UTC
URL: https://www.cell.com/cell/fulltext/S0092-8674(26)00924-4?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Gut bacteria convert inorganic nitrate and non-heme iron into bioactive dinitrosyl iron complexes (DNICs) that distribute systemically and signal independently of free nitric oxide. These microbe-derived DNICs protect against cardiometabolic disease and fatty liver by restoring compromised metabolic signaling pathways.

Source: The Lancet
Published: 2026-10-26 20:15 UTC
URL: https://www.thelancet.com/journals/lancet/article/PIIS0140-6736(26)01293-6/fulltext?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

The digital determinants of health are the structural conditions through which digital infrastructures, business models, and design choices shape health outcomes. Yet these determinants remain largely outside public health policy and remain fragmented across regulatory sectors. Rather than regulating individual technologies in isolation, governance should ensure that digital systems and environments advance population health and reduce inequities. A public health approach shifts attention from individual responsibility—and the tendency to blame users—towards the governance of the structural features that produce health risks and inequities.

### [Series] The public health challenges of children's and adolescents’ digital media use: applying the digital determinants of health as an interpretive framework
Source: The Lancet
Published: 2026-10-26 20:15 UTC
URL: https://www.thelancet.com/journals/lancet/article/PIIS0140-6736(26)01611-9/fulltext?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Children and adolescents are growing up in a hybrid world in which digital media has become part of the infrastructure of everyday life and a structural determinant of their health. In the second paper of this Series on the digital determinants of health, we apply the framework introduced in the first paper to one of the most contested questions in public health: how young people's digital media use shapes their health and wellbeing. Using the digital determinants of health framework to interpret the current evidence base, we examine what is known, and what remains poorly understood, about the public health implications of children's and adolescents’ digital media use.

### [Series] The digital determinants of health: a new era for global public health
Source: The Lancet
Published: 2026-10-26 20:15 UTC
URL: https://www.thelancet.com/journals/lancet/article/PIIS0140-6736(26)01292-4/fulltext?rss=yes
Source meta: journal · primary · R5 · P5 · baseline

Digital transformations exert increasing influence over the lives of the global population, shaping opportunities and generating harms for health and wellbeing across the spectrum of digital access and engagement. The scale of influence of digital transformations on human and planetary health marks the urgency of attention to the digital determinants of health (DDoH). In this Series paper, we describe a prevailing digital sociotechnological order (DSTO) as the structural source of the DDoH. The DSTO is composed of a set of dominant assumptions that motivate digital practices operating through four features (political norms, economic logics, infrastructures, and personal and social practices) to affect human and planetary health.

### Hybrid SEIR Temporal Fusion Transformer Framework with SHAP Analysis for Interpretable Pandemic Forecasting
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-26 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31673
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.31673v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: Forecasting pandemic spread across heterogeneous regions demands models that are both accurate and interpretable for public health action. This paper proposes a hybrid forecasting framework that integrates an SEIR epidemiological model enhanced with a Temporal Fusion Transformer (TFT) to produce interpretable multi-horizon infection forecasts. Using region-wise COVID-19 data from the Our World in Data corpus, we present a unified pipeline that integrates epidemiological indicators (susceptible, exposed, infectious, recovered), intervention signals (masking, vaccination, policies), mobility covariates, and demographic context. The SEIR component estimates latent disease dynamics and injects mechanistic priors, while the TFT learns non-linear temporal dependencies and cross-feature interactions from historical observations and known future inputs.

### Economic Corridor Detection from Multi-Source Earth Observation and Commercial Geospatial Data Across Nigeria's Kilometre-Scale Grid
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-26 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31675
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.31675v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: Identifying economic corridors at national scale is challenging where firm registries, transaction flows, and local economic-output statistics are incomplete or unavailable. This paper develops a reproducible multi-proxy screening framework for detecting economic-corridor candidates across Nigeria using a kilometre-scale analytical grid. The framework combines built-up land cover from ESA WorldCover, night-time light radiance from NASA VIIRS Black Marble, building-footprint density from Google Open Buildings, a static urban-intensity proxy derived from population, built-up share and night-time light level, and commercial point-of-interest density from Overture Places and OpenStreetMap. A dual proxy gate requiring both built-up evidence, defined as built-up fraction above 0.1, and night-time luminosity, defined as radiance above 1 nW cm-2 sr-1, identifies active corridor cells, which are then clustered into corridor objects and assigned interpretable typologies.

### Gravitational Lensing of the Bullet Cluster in the CCC+TL Cosmology: A Controlled Proof-of-Concept Study
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-26 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31735
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.31735v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: A proof-of-concept study of gravitational lensing in the Bullet Cluster under Covarying Coupling Constants plus Tired Light (CCC+TL) cosmology. Using a JWST spectroscopic catalogue of 219 cluster members and 135 gold multiple images in 49 systems from Rihtarsic et al. (2026), we construct convergence maps for CCC+TL and a simplified $\Lambda$CDM model using their respective angular-diameter distances and critical surface densities. The CCC+TL model adopts a two-parameter effective-density prescription anchored to the same virial-scale mass budget as $\Lambda$CDM, rather than fitted to multiple-image positions. The CCC+TL and $\Lambda$CDM mean-convergence bands in radial rings overlap within the propagated $\pm 30 \%$ virial-mass uncertainty across all rings containing multiple images ($\theta\leq80\arcsec$).

Published: 2026-10-26 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31757
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.31757v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: Programmable photonic meshes can perform general linear transformations, but universal transformations on $N$ modes require $O(N^2)$ programmable phase shifters, which cannot simultaneously achieve low drive voltage, gigahertz speed, zero static power, lossless modulation, and a compact footprint. Signal routing does not require universal transformations, which allows considerable circuit simplifications. We present a compact reflective circuit architecture that connects all input-output pairs across $N$ modes in $N$ steps using only $O(N)$ programmable phase shifters. The circuit comprises a single passive $N\times N$ interferometer and a single column of programmable reflective phase shifters. We demonstrate the architecture by implementing a $3\times 3$ router in x-cut thin-film lithium niobate with a passive multimode interferometer and phase shifters based on single-sided photonic-crystal nanobeam cavities. ### Plasma morphology under nanosecond repetitively pulsed discharges with air cross-flow: A simulation study
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-26 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31773
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.31773v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: Nanosecond repetitively pulsed discharges in air cross-flows exhibit distinct plasma morphologies that can expand the spatial extent of bulk plasma regions and increase the residence time of reactive radicals within the flow field. This work develops a high-fidelity plasma-fluid simulation framework within OpenFOAM that couples drift-diffusion-convection transport, electrostatic potential, photoionization, mixture-averaged transport, ambipolar diffusion, and a local mean electron energy formulation. Using this framework, we systematically investigate plasma morphologies under air cross-flow velocities of 40 and 100 m/s at a repetition frequency of 50 kHz. The simulations show that lower cross-flow velocities promote plasma retention within previously formed channels, resulting in substantial thermal accumulation. In contrast, at higher cross-flow velocities, plasma is rapidly convected downstream, reducing retention between pulses and weakening thermal accumulation.

### Overview of Enhanced D-alpha and Quasi Continuous Exhaust H-modes at DIII-D
Source: arXiv
Published: 2026-10-26 04:00 UTC
URL: https://arxiv.org/abs/2610.31782
Source meta: preprint · primary · R4 · P5 · signal

arXiv:2610.31782v1 Announce Type: new Abstract: We review the Enhanced D-alpha (EDA) and Quasi-continuous Exhaust (QCE) H-modes that have recently been achieved in the DIII-D tokamak. These scenarios are free of large type-I edge-localized modes (ELMs) and achieved at high fueling compatible with divertor detachment. This makes them promising operational regimes as ELMs are a major risk for fusion pilot plants (FPPs). Access to EDA H-mode and the associated quasi-coherent mode (QCM) was observed at low heating power barely above the L-H power threshold, and increased density enabled access to EDA H-mode even at higher heating powers. The QCE H-mode was achieved at simultaneously high density and heating power, characterized also by a QCM and small ELMs with transient peaks of the total power into the divertor below about 3MW, in line with observations at ASDEX Upgrade. During the QCE H-mode, the turbulence parameter alpha_t at the separatrix is about 1 and does not separate ELMy or EDA from QCE H-modes well.

### Scientists Can Now Spot Hidden Sunspots on the Solar Far Side
Source: Universe Today
Published: 2026-10-26 18:39 UTC
URL: https://www.universetoday.com/articles/scientists-can-now-spot-hidden-sunspots-on-the-solar-far-side
Source meta: specialist_media · specialist · R4 · P4 · baseline

Unlike the Moon, the Sun is constantly rotating from our perspective - but we still only see half of it at any given time. Since it takes 27 days to complete a full rotation, that leaves plenty of time for changes to happen to the side we can’t see that could directly impact Earth’s own space weather a few weeks later. Now a team of physicists led by Bibhuti Kumar Jha of the Southwest Research Institute has published a paper in The Astrophysical Journal Supplement Series that uses probability to map out what might be happening on the far side of the Sun, even if we can’t see it directly.
